Gold supporting: angiosperms synthesize s - methylmethionine ( smm ) from methionine ( met ) and s - adenosylmethionine ( adomet ) in a unique reaction catalyzed by met s - methyltransferase ( mmt ).
LLM supporting:  the s - adenosylmethionine ( smm ) cycle, which involves the methyl donor s - adenosylmethionine, is a well - established mechanism in angiosperms for regulating the metabolism of methionine ( met ) and s - adenosylmethionine. the key enzymes in this cycle are homocysteine s - methyltransferase ( hmt ) and methionine synthase ( mmt ). hmt catalyzes the transfer of a methyl group from smm to homocysteine, forming methionine. mmt, on the other hand, uses smm as a methyl donor to synthesize s - adenosylmethionine from homocysteine. this cycle helps to maintain the balance of met and smm, preventing the depletion of free met and the accumulation of smm. the smm cycle is active in various tissues of plants, including leaves, roots, and developing seeds, and it is essential for the regulation of adomet levels, which are crucial for many cellular processes, including histone methylation. therefore, the s - adenosylmethionine ( smm ) is the methyl donor for histone methyltransferases in plants.
